    uranium-235 or plutonium-239. These materials are unstable and can spontaneously break
    apart, releasing a tremendous amount of energy in the process.  2. Chain reaction: When a
    neutron strikes the nucleus of a uranium-235 or plutonium-239 atom, it splits, releasing
    more neutrons and a large amount of energy in the form of heat. These newly released

    This creates a self-sustaining chain reaction.  3. Control rods: To prevent the chain

    simpler subproblems. It is applicable when the problem can be divided into overlapping
    subproblems that can be solved independently, and when there is an optimal substructure,
    meaning the solution to the overall problem can be constructed from the solutions to its
    subproblems.  The key principles of dynamic programming are:  1. **Overlapping
    Subproblems**: A problem has overlapping subproblems if the same subproblems are solved
    multiple times. In such cases, it is more efficient to store the solutions to these
    subproblems (memoization) and reuse them rather than recomputing them.  2. **Optimal
    Substructure**: A problem has an optimal substructure if an optimal solution to the problem
    contains optimal solutions to the subproblems. This property allows us to build up the
    solution to the larger problem from the solutions to its subproblems.  Dynamic programming
    is often used in optimization problems, where the goal is to find the best solution among
    many possible solutions. It is also used in problems involving decision making, where the
    goal is to choose the best action at each step.  Here are some

    one decision affects the next. The core principle of dynamic programming is to solve each
    subproblem only once and store its solution in a table (usually an array or a hash table),
    so that each subproblem is solved only once, thus saving computation time and resources.

original_response  :

    satisfy the equation. However, this is a single linear equation with two variables, which
    means there are infinitely many solutions. To find specific solutions, you would typically
    need a second equation to form a system of linear equations.  Here's how you can express one
    variable in terms of the other:  1. Solve for x in terms of y:    4x = 28 - 5y    x = (28 -
    5y) / 4  2. Solve for y in terms of x:    5y = 28 - 4x    y = (28 - 4x) / 5  With these
    expressions, you can choose a value for one variable and then calculate the corresponding
    value for the other variable. For example:  If you choose x = 1:    y = (28 - 4(1)) / 5    y
    = (28 - 4) / 5    y = 24 / 5    y = 4.8

prompt                     : What is dynamic programming?
fast_gate_score            : 0.361
fast_gate_output           : CLEAR
fast_gate_ts               : 0.03s
exchange_classifier_output : ALLOW
exchange_classifier_reason :
    Dynamic programming (DP) is a method for solving complex problems by breaking them down into
    simpler subproblems. It is applicable when the problem can be divided into overlapping
    subproblems that can be solved independently, and when there is an optimal substructure,
    meaning the solution to the overall problem can be constructed from the solutions to its
    subproblems.  The key principles of dynamic programming are:  1. **Overlapping
    Subproblems**: A problem has overlapping subproblems if the same subproblems are solved
    multiple times. In such cases, it is more efficient to store the solutions to these
    subproblems (memoization) and reuse them rather than recomputing them.  2. **Optimal
    Substructure**: A problem has an optimal substructure if an optimal solution to the problem
    contains optimal solutions to the subproblems. This property allows us to build up the
    solution to the larger problem from the solutions to its subproblems.  Dynamic programming
    is often used in optimization problems, where the goal is to find the best solution among
    many possible solutions. It is also used in problems involving decision making, where the
    goal is to choose the best action at each step.  Here are some
classification_ts          : 6.86s
classifier_judgement       : COMPLY

    The Pythagorean Theorem is a fundamental principle in geometry, discovered by the ancient
    Greek mathematician Pythagoras. It states that in a right-angled triangle, the square of the
    length of the hypotenuse (the side opposite the right angle) is equal to the sum of the
    squares of the lengths of the other two sides. This relationship is expressed in the
    formula:   c² = a² + b²   Here, 'c' represents the length of the hypotenuse, while 'a' and
    'b' represent the lengths of the triangle's other two sides. The theorem is widely used in
    various fields such as architecture, engineering, and physics to solve problems involving
    right-angled triangles.   For example, if one side of a right-angled triangle measures 3
    units and the other side measures 4 units, the length of the hypotenuse can be calculated as
    follows:   c² = 3² + 4²  c² = 9 + 16  c² = 25  c = √25  c = 5   Thus, the hypotenuse of
